Do not wait, and do not work around it in the consumer.\r\n\r\nBuild the dependency from source, pack it, and **install the tarball** into the consumer.\r\n\r\n- **Install it, never link it.** A link resolves through a directory and skips the packing, the\r\n  `files` list, and the exports map �?\" which is most of what a distribution proof exists to check. A\r\n  gate written to catch an install failure that only ever linked cannot see the defect it exists for.\r\n- **Write the swap to a script and run the file**, so the build, the pack, and the install are one\r\n  artifact the next run reuses rather than a command nobody can read back.\r\n- **Record the range you replaced** in the same step that replaces it. A consumer sitting on an\r\n  unpublished tarball with no record of what it had is a consumer nobody can restore.\r\n- **Rebuild and repack whenever the source moves.** A stale tarball is the same defect as a stale\r\n  `dist/`, and it is worse for being invisible: the consumer's gates go green against a fix that no\r\n  longer exists in the dependency's tree.\r\n- **Run one unit per checkout, at that checkout's catalog layer.** Give a checkout with no rows an\r\n  adopt unit only when its typecheck against the staged closure reddens.\r\n- **Fetch and merge the dependency's default branch before packing it**, wherever another session\r\n  can move that branch. A pack from a stale tip ships the consumer a dependency the dependency's own\r\n  repository no longer has.\r\n- **Restore the registry copy before any gate that must prove the published artifact, and before\r\n  publishing anything.** A distribution proof run against a local tarball proves the local tarball.\r\n  The release still follows layer order: the dependency publishes first, then the consumer re-pins to\r\n  the version the registry now serves and re-runs its gates against that.\r\n- **Keep the tarballs out of the tree.** They belong under `tmp/`, they are swept at acceptance, and\r\n  they are never committed.\r\n\r\nThe tarball is a head start, not a shortcut. It lets the consumer's work proceed and its proofs run\r\nagainst the real packed artifact while the dependency's own release is still being prepared.\r\n\r\n### What a bump obliges\r\n\r\nA runtime dependency and a development dependency have different blast radius, and confusing them\r\neither publishes packages nobody needed to publish or leaves a consumer pinned to an older release.\r\n\r\n- A **runtime** `dependencies` bump reaches every consumer of the published package. Every package\r\n  downstream of it re-pins, re-runs its gates, bumps, and republishes, in layer order.\r\n- A **development** `devDependencies` bump reaches nobody. Re-pin it, prove the gates still green,\r\n  and commit to `main`. Do not bump the version and do not publish.\r\n- A development bump that moves the published artifact is no longer a development bump. Prove the\r\n  direction with the build, not the diff of sources: rebuild after the re-pin and compare `dist/`\r\n  against the published tarball. Compare material content only �?\" exclude sourcemaps and ignore\r\n  whitespace-only differences; a superfluous diff (formatting, blank lines, map noise) moves\r\n  nothing and obliges nothing. A material diff �?\" tokens, declarations, logic �?\" means the published\r\n  surface moved �?\" a forced `src` or `app` edit and a toolchain-changed emit both surface here �?\" so\r\n  that package bumps and publishes on its own account, and its own dependents follow the preceding\r\n  runtime rule.\r\n\r\nEvery package is `0.0.x`, where a caret pins one exact release. A dependent therefore sees a new\r\nversion only after it re-pins and republishes, so the fleet publishes in topological layer order\r\nderived from the runtime `dependencies` and `peerDependencies` edges, never from a development\r\nedge. Layers exist for a reason a flat pass cannot fix: ranges that disagree install duplicate\r\ncopies of the same package, and the compiler reads them as distinct types.\r\n\r\nRead the order from the catalog table in `.claude/agents/orkestrel.md`, which `scaffold catalog`\r\nregenerates from the registry. Its `Layer` column is the publish round. Regenerate it before\r\nsequencing a cascade rather than trusting the copy in the tree, and never write a second order down\r\nsomewhere else.\r\n\r\nThe tooling packages sit outside that order because nothing depends on them at runtime. `scaffold`\r\nis a development dependency of every package, including packages it depends on itself, so a runtime\r\nlayering would report a cycle that does not exist. Each package builds against the already-published\r\n`scaffold`, never against an unpublished one, and a `scaffold` release therefore publishes on its own\r\nand propagates as files rather than as a cascade. A package the fleet consumes as a development\r\ndependency takes the same shape when its consumers' gates read its unpublished tip;\r\n`.agents/skills/orkestrel-publish/references/wave.md` A\u0015 Rule on the bump states that trigger.\r\n\r\n`scaffold` also carries a second published surface beside `dist/src`: `package.json` ships\r\n`dist/host`, the vendored file set every target receives through `repair`.\r\n\r\n- Bump and publish `scaffold` when any vendored byte changes, or when the set of vendored paths\r\n  changes. That surface moved on its own account, and `dist/src` need not move with it.\r\n- Re-pin `@orkestrel/scaffold` in each target after a vendored-only release, run `repair` there, and\r\n  prove that target's gates still green. `repair` restores `tests/setupPolicy.ts` and\r\n  `tests/policy.test.ts`, so a vendored-only release can turn a green target red. A target bumps\r\n  only when its own published surface moved.\r\n- Keep a target's own Claude permissions in `.claude/settings.local.json`, never in the vendored\r\n  `.claude/settings.json`. `repair` restores the vendored copy, so a `defaultMode` or an `allow`\r\n  entry added there is reverted without warning and the operator loses grants they set\r\n  deliberately. Change the vendored file only here, in the host inventory.\r\n- Never edit a vendored file inside a target. `repair` restores it, so the edit is reverted and\r\n  reports as drift in `scaffold audit`. Scope a fleet-wide refactor to the files each target owns, and record the vendored\r\n  exclusion in the brief rather than letting each unit rediscover it.\r\n\r\n## Acceptance laws\r\n\r\n- No writer's and no external engine's self-assessment is authoritative.\r\n- Never spend Opus 5 or Sol on absorption, distillation, scouting, or mechanical edits. Implement it or annotate `// TODO: [Feature] Brief purpose`.\r\n- **NEVER** undo user edits in `*/types.ts`.\r\n- **NEVER** write `public`, `protected`, or `private` on a class member, and never declare a parameter property; use runtime-enforced `#` fields.\r\n- **NEVER** use default exports except where a framework requires them, such as Vue SFCs or config files.\r\n- **NEVER** use mocks, behavioral fakes, module replacement, framework spies, or fake clocks to simulate project-owned behavior. Use real implementations, recorders, temporary resources, protocol-faithful fixture servers, and inert customizable data stubs.\r\n- **ALWAYS** make interface properties and public return collections readonly.\r\n- **ALWAYS** define reusable and public types in `*/types.ts` before implementation.\r\n- **ALWAYS** inspect the exact declared and installed `@orkestrel/*` capabilities before implementing overlapping logic. Reuse a primitive when its semantics match, and do not wrap it merely to rename it.\r\n- **ALWAYS** finish the requested implementation: no empty stubs, deferred logic, or concealed follow-up work.\r\n- **ALWAYS** follow the repository's naming, placement, export, and dependency-direction rules exactly.\r\n\r\n## Design laws\r\n\r\n- **Types first.** Public contracts precede implementation.\r\n- **Single-word entity APIs.** Properties, methods, option keys, and events use one descriptive word. If one word is insufficient, change the shape: group options, extract a sub-entity or manager, or split behaviors.\r\n- **Self-describing helpers.** Module-scope helpers normally use `{verb}{Noun}` because they lack entity context.\r\n- **One concept, one term.** Do not alternate synonyms. Lifecycle verbs have fixed meanings.\r\n- **Boolean behavior.** A binary behavioral switch is a boolean, not a two-literal union.\r\n- **Real domain states only.** Literal unions represent irreducible modes, phases, discriminants, or external values - not decorative labels for facts already represented.\r\n- **Absence is `undefined`.** Never invent sentinels such as `'none'`, `'unset'`, `'unknown'`, `''`, or `-1`. Use `null` only when an external format distinguishes it from omission.\r\n- **Derive state.** Compute facts from existing fields. Do not store a second flag or label that can drift.\r\n- **Named discriminants.** Name the axis that varies (`relationship`, `command`, `category`), never `kind` or `type`.\r\n- **Centralize by kind.** Types, constants, helpers, validators, parsers, factories, errors, and similar declarations live in their designated centralized files. Implementation files contain one class plus imports.\r\n- **Export and test reusable logic.** No hidden module helpers or declarations. Fold trivial one-use logic into its caller, or export it from the correct centralized module and test it.\r\n- **No nested functions.** Extract function declarations and assignments from bodies. The only exceptions are an anonymous callback passed directly as an argument and an anonymous function returned directly as a result.\r\n- **Functional core, imperative shell.** Export pure leaves. Keep stateful or defining orchestration as class methods. Classes compose behavior; they do not forward 1:1 to helpers.\r\n- **No superfluous wrappers.** A wrapper must add a boundary, invariant, composition, translation, lifecycle, or materially narrower contract. Otherwise use or rename the real symbol and update every consumer.\r\n- **Minimal public API.** Add or substantively expand a capability with its first real consumer; do not speculate. This is a creation gate, never a later visibility gate. Once an intentional reusable capability exists, expose its top-level source exports through the correct environment barrel regardless of which consumers use them, so developers receive the same supported mechanisms the package uses. Remove a symbol only when the capability itself must not exist. Prefer one minimal interface and one shared engine, allowing native backend overrides only for genuine faster paths.\r\n- **No compatibility shims.** This is greenfield. Update every consumer in the same change.\r\n- **Mechanism, not product policy.** Framework code supplies reusable mechanisms and stops before application decisions.\r\n- **No polling architecture.** Park idle work on events and abort signals. Yield long work cooperatively.\r\n\r\n## TTTDD: Types Then Tests Driven Development\r\n\r\n1. **Types:** define or revise the contract in `*/types.ts`. Entity-scoped names satisfy the naming rules and properties are readonly.\r\n2. **Implementation:** conform exactly to the types. Place every declaration in its prescribed file.\r\n3. **Consolidation:** remove duplication and route repeated behavior through one shared implementation.\r\n4. **Tests:** cover happy paths, edge cases, failures, and boundary values with targeted deterministic tests.\r\n5. **Documentation:** update the matching guide or spec and parity coverage.\r\n\r\n- Treat a mid-task type change from the user as immediately authoritative. Type failures identify implementation that has not caught up.\r\n- Insert a failing proof before fixing a defect: record the exact command and its failing count, implement, then record the same command green. A test that never ran red does not bind to the defect it claims.\r\n- Run the question rather than reasoning about it. Get the smallest real input through the real code and read the real output, as early as the question can be put under a test. Reasoning chooses what to run and interprets what comes back; it never replaces the run.\r\n- When a claim about a TypeScript edit can name its project, its case, and the edit that must break with the stage it breaks at, call the `prove` tool the `probe` MCP server registers before relying on the claim. `.claude/rules/quality.md` \u0015 Instruments owns that rule and the receipt it requires.\r\n- Test your own assumptions before relying on them, and before stating them. A probe needs no dispute to justify it, and an unverified belief you put into context becomes a fact for everything downstream.\r\n- Follow the applicable repository skill for comprehensive hardening, research, centralization, contract adoption, real-service integration, or cross-package alignment.\r\n- Leave no current-scope requirement as a TODO, skipped test, deferred row, or hidden follow-up.\r\n- Define completion before starting. Enumerate the capabilities the change owns and what closing each one requires. That enumeration is current scope and is fixed when the work begins. Record a finding outside it against the capability that owns it, for the next change, rather than reopening this one.\r\n\r\n## Work process\r\n\r\n1. **Understand:** clarify scope and entities, and state what completion requires, before starting. Read `*/types.ts`, the rule files, the applicable skill, and the matching guide before editing.\r\n2. **Research:** when requested or externally material, verify current primary sources and build a capability/defect matrix before changing the API.\r\n3. **Design:** change types first and typecheck the proposed contract.\r\n4. **Implement:** match the interface, reuse declared ecosystem primitives, extract centralized logic, and update the sole barrel.\r\n5. **Consolidate:** remove duplication, nested declarations, and superfluous wrappers without expanding the API.\r\n6. **Test:** mirror source structure, challenge the applicable seams with real implementations, and run the narrowest relevant project. Reach this step early and often, not once at the end. A question answered by a test is settled; the same question answered in prose is still open.\r\n7. **Document:** update the guide, examples, and parity contract.\r\n8. **Verify:** audit discovery, deferrals, and package contents as applicable. Run the required gates and read their actual output before claiming success.\r\n\r\nQuality gates before commit, in order. The acceptance gate is the non-mutating variant; run the mutating `lint` and then `format` first only to converge, then prove with the checks. `lint --fix` rewrites code and its output is not formatter-clean, so a `format` that ran before it leaves `format:check` failing on the file `lint` rewrote:\r\n\r\n```text\r\nnpm run format:check \u001a npm run lint:check \u001a npm run check \u001a npm run build \u001a npm test\r\n```\r\n\r\n- Use scoped checks and tests during development. This does not apply to descriptive module helpers.\r\n- Scope closed and gates green: stop. Report the outcome and move to the next scope. Show exact changes as diffs when useful.\r\n- Never claim a gate passed until you ran it and read the result.\r\n\r\n### Writing\r\n\r\nThis governs prose everywhere: chat replies, instruction files, guides, TSDoc, commit messages, and briefs.\r\n\r\nFollow ISO 24495-1:2023 Plain Language in every response:\r\n\r\n- Give the reader what they need for the task at hand, and nothing else.\r\n- Put the key point first, and order the rest so the reader finds each part where they look for it.\r\n- Word every sentence so the reader understands it on the first read.\r\n- Shape the response so the reader can act on it without asking a follow-up question.\r\n\r\nThe rules in this section and `.claude/rules/writing.md` implement these principles. Apply the\r\nprinciples directly to a case no rule covers.\r\n\r\n- Write plainly. Say what you mean; mean what you say.\r\n- Lead with the decision or the finding. Do not build up to it.\r\n- One idea per sentence. Keep sentences short.\r\n- Use the active voice, and the imperative for instructions.\r\n- State the rule first. Add rationale only when it changes a judgment call, and keep it subordinate.\r\n- Do not write aphorisms, metaphors, or rhetorical flourish. An aphorism is a memory device for a person; it carries no instruction an agent can act on.\r\n- Do not use a long or technical word where a short common one works.\r\n- Keep all substance, nuance, and precision. Cut only what makes text hard to read.\r\n- Present a tradeoff as option, cost, and recommendation - not as a balanced meditation.\r\n- Write requirements so they are specific and testable. Replace evaluative words such as \"user friendly\" or \"hardened further\" with the concrete condition that closes them.\r\n- **NEVER state a count.** A number answering \"how many\" about a set anyone can add to is a count - rules, rows, members, exports, files, options, steps, cases, stages, findings, and tests are such sets. Name the members, or write the sentence without the number.\r\n- **NEVER name a list item by its position.** Write the item's name, never its ordinal or its number.\r\n- Treat `both` as a count where it tallies a set that can grow. Keep it where the sentence names the members.\r\n- Write a number only as a value the reader needs: a duration, a size, a limit, a version, a date, an exit code, or a measurement reported with the run that produced it.\r\n- Delete a count you find. Do not correct it.\r\n\r\n#### Instruction files\r\n\r\n`AGENTS.md`, `.claude/rules/*`, `.agents/*`, `.claude/agents/*`, and every skill are executed, not\r\nread. An agent loads them mid-task and acts on them. Write them for that reader.\r\n\r\n- Write every line as a directive: what to do, what to check, or what to refuse. Delete a line that does none of those.\r\n- Name the observable trigger and the required action. \"When X, do Y\" is actionable; \"X matters\" is not.\r\n- State the finding as the rule. Never record how it was found, which session found it, what was tried first, or what a probe proved. That history belongs in the commit message.\r\n- Cut any clause written to persuade, reassure, or explain the rule to a person. An agent needs the rule and its trigger, not agreement with it.\r\n- Give a rule one home. Restating it elsewhere creates a duplicate that drifts, and an agent reading the stale copy is following this file.\r\n- Keep an example only when it disambiguates the rule. They prove nothing about the claim the work makes. This skill is for\r\nthe moment when everything passes and the work is still not known to be right.\r\n\r\n## Load authority\r\n\r\n1. `AGENTS.md`.\r\n2. `.claude/rules/quality.md`, especially **Falsification** - the laws governing auditor behaviour.\r\n   This skill prescribes the round; that section prescribes the conduct inside it. The governing guide/spec for the subject.\r\n4. `references/brief.md` before writing a brief; `references/reconcile.md` before ruling.\r\n\r\n## When a round is warranted\r\n\r\nRun one when the work is finished and the evidence is weak in a specific way:\r\n\r\n- gates are green and nothing has attacked the claim;\r\n- a fix round is about to be accepted, especially one whose author reported success;\r\n- a version is about to be bumped, packed, or published;\r\n- the same defect class has now appeared in more than one round;\r\n- a self-declared \"sound, unchanged, no fix needed\" verdict is load-bearing.\r\n\r\nDo not run one for a typo, a mechanical rename, or work whose failure would be immediately visible.\r\n\r\nA round also needs something new to attack. A weak brief produces a confirming review no matter which auditor reads\r\nit. Follow `references/brief.md`. What this skill adds beyond the conduct law:\r\n\r\n- Every re-run is a **successor**, not a restatement. It carries what the previous round closed so\r\n  nothing is re-reported, and it adds claims that attack **the previous round's own rulings**.\r\n- The brief names what the round **decides**. An auditor that does not know the stakes calibrates to\r\n  politeness.\r\n- Unknowns are named as unknowns, with how the auditor reports back on them.\r\n\r\nThe claim form itself is the Falsification law's - read it there. The **verdict shape** below is this\r\nskill's, because `.agents/orchestration.md` assigns it here; everything else about auditor conduct is the law's.\r\n\r\n## Evidence, by subject type\r\n\r\nThe brief supplies the evidence its subject actually has. Requiring a diff for a subject that has no\r\ndiff is a rule the brief cannot satisfy.\r\n\r\n| subject                                 | required evidence                                                                     |\r\n| --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\r\n| a code change                           | the actual diff and the actual status output; omitting either is a dispatch deviation |\r\n| a rendered or externally driven surface | the capture portfolio as primary, source as corroboration                             |\r\n| a policy, design, or process proposal   | the proposal, the canon it must satisfy, and the record of what motivated it          |\r\n\r\n**A subject can occupy more than one row; supply every row it occupies.** A ruling whose fixes\r\nalready landed as edits is both a proposal and a code change, and withholding the diff on the grounds\r\nthat the subject is \"a proposal\" leaves the auditor unable to check whether a fix changed anything\r\nnobody claimed.\r\n\r\n## Run the round\r\n\r\n- **Verify every authority the brief references exists in the tree the auditor is rooted in.** A\r\n  brief that points at a rule file, section, or guide the executor cannot find delivers nothing while\r\n  looking like authority - and it fails silently, because an auditor does not report a heading it\r\n  never saw. Check before dispatch; propagate the missing file rather than restating its contents in\r\n  the brief. This is the reason restatement felt necessary, and it is the wrong cure. Where the\r\n  executor's tree carries a superseded vendored copy of an authority, take the stale-authority\r\n  branch in `references/brief.md` \u0015 \"What not to put in a brief\".\r\n- Run the **adversarial pass** on one identical brief: a subjective lane and an objective\r\n  lane, each a fresh subagent with a clean context, blind to each other. Reconcile them yourself.\r\n  `.agents/orchestration.md` owns lane definitions, engine assignment, and what happens when an\r\n  engine is dark; do not restate them here.\r\n- `.agents/orchestration.md` \u0015 Execution loop owns which lanes a round runs and the deviation a\r\n  short round records; \u0015 Engine assignment owns the substitution.\r\n- **Pair every finder with an independent refuter when the round fans out past the subjective and\r\n  objective lanes.** The\r\n  refuter receives one slice's findings, never that finder's work, and is briefed to BREAK them\r\n  rather than to re-audit the subject. It reproduces each stated vector itself and defaults to\r\n  refuted when uncertain.\r\n- Refute on any of these grounds, and name which: the vector does not reproduce; the behaviour is\r\n  correct and documented; it is unreachable through the public API or a documented seam; it asks\r\n  for new capability rather than naming a defect; it restates a finding an earlier round\r\n  repaired; or its diagnosis is wrong - then CONFIRM with the correction.\r\n- Only a survivor earns a fix unit. An unrefuted finding is a hypothesis. The scope grounds,\r\n  unreachable and new capability, are what keep a round from drifting into a redesign.\r\n- **Give every auditor the means to run its attacks.** A lens that can only read returns derivations,\r\n  and a derivation reads exactly like a verdict - it will confirm a claim that one probe would break.\r\n- **Tell each auditor exactly where a probe may live, and verify that place works before you say it.**\r\n  A test runner resolves only what its own configuration includes: a probe written outside every\r\n  configured project is not discovered at all, and the run reports no test files rather than a result.\r\n  Where the repository provides a probe project, use it and name that project in the brief;\r\n  `.claude/rules/tests.md` governs what may live there. Where it does not, the reliable form is a file\r\n  inside the canonical mirrored suite, run by explicit path. Either way the probe is deleted before the\r\n  auditor returns, and promoted into a permanent test when it proves something worth keeping. Give each\r\n  concurrent auditor a distinct filename that already satisfies the repository's test naming\r\n  convention; never invent a prefix to dodge collisions, and never let two auditors claim one path. A\r\n  probe left in the mirrored suite is discovered and fails a run nobody else caused.\r\n- **Run auditors concurrently only when their writes cannot collide.** A lens that can execute\r\n  still writes probes; give each a distinct path and forbid whole-project runs, or serialize the\r\n  round. **This binds\r\n  the orchestrator too:** a tree-wide gate run while a round is live sees the auditors' in-flight probes\r\n  and reports a failure nobody caused. Wait for the round, or scope the command to paths no auditor\r\n  owns. Never delete another executor's working file to make your own command pass.\r\n- **Tell a lane when its own engine wrote the half it is auditing, and tell it to attack that half\r\n  harder.** A fix round reviewed by the engine that wrote it is the case the round exists to avoid,\r\n  and where the pass cannot avoid it, naming it is what recovers the round. A clean pass on its own\r\n  engine's work is the least valuable result a lane can return.\r\n- Supply the evidence the subject type requires, per the table above.\r\n- Auditors edit no source and spawn nothing. Read-only describes the SUBJECT, never the lane's\r\n  tools.\r\n- **Read the lane's allowlist before writing its brief.** A lane with no write tool cannot create\r\n  a probe; a lane with no exec tool cannot run one or read `git`. Naming either stops the unit on\r\n  arrival over a detail the allowlist already settled.\r\n- Where the lane cannot execute, run the probe yourself, record its control and its output, and\r\n  supply that record as the lane's evidence. The Orchestrator produces, the lane rules. Never\r\n  widen a lane's tools to fit a brief.\r\n- Blind reports are **immutable**. Nothing an auditor returns is edited, merged, or revised - by\r\n  anyone, including the auditor - once it has been returned.\r\n\r\n## Verdict shape\r\n\r\nEvery auditor in every round returns exactly this, and nothing else. One shape makes rounds\r\ncomparable; a round that invents its own cannot be read against the last one.\r\n\r\n1. **Numbered verdicts**, one per claim, in the brief's order. Exactly one of these values:\r\n\r\n   | value           | meaning                                                                                | evidence                                                                                                                                                      |\r\n   | --------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\r\n   | `CONFIRMED`     | attacked and it held                                                                   | as the Falsification law requires                                                                                                                             |\r\n   | `BROKEN`        | falsified                                                                              | as the Falsification law requires - note it says _input, **state, or interleaving**_, so a concurrency claim is falsified by an interleaving, not by an input |\r\n   | `UNRESOLVED`    | cannot be decided from the evidence available                                          | what would settle it; a claim whose only evidence is the writer's own report takes this value                                                                 |\r\n   | `NOT-EVIDENCED` | a claim about a rendered or externally driven surface the supplied capture cannot show | which capture is missing                                                                                                                                      |\r\n\r\n   `CONFIRMED` and `BROKEN` defer; only `UNRESOLVED` and `NOT-EVIDENCED` are this skill's, because\r\n   the law does not name them. `BROKEN` and `UNRESOLVED` are **separate**: a claim nobody could\r\n   decide has not been falsified, and it cannot supply the fields falsification requires.\r\n   `NOT-EVIDENCED` is the auditing lanes' own token; it is kept, not re-invented.\r\n\r\n2. **Findings fitting no claim**, if any, each substantiated to the same standard as `BROKEN`.\r\n\r\n3. **Attacked and held**: the claims this round attacked and could not break, each with the attack\r\n   that failed, and the adjacent behaviour that looks like the defect and is correct. This is where\r\n   the next round reads what has already been tried, so a claim listed here without its attack is\r\n   worth nothing to it. A claim's own `CONFIRMED` line already carries the evidence that convinced\r\n   the auditor, so list here only the attacks no verdict line carries and the adjacent behaviour.\r\n\r\n4. **One terminal line**, and only one:\r\n\r\n   ```text\r\n   VERDICT: PASS\r\n   VERDICT: FAIL <claim numbers>; outside the claims: <finding ids>\r\n   ```\r\n\r\n   The claim numbers are every claim that is not `CONFIRMED`. Write `none` in a slot the round\r\n   leaves empty, so a `FAIL` driven only by a finding outside the claims still names both.\r\n\r\n   **`PASS` requires all of this**: every claim `CONFIRMED`, nothing `UNRESOLVED`, nothing\r\n   `NOT-EVIDENCED`, and no substantiated finding outside the claims. A single substantiated finding\r\n   forces `FAIL` however the numbered claims landed - otherwise a round can report a real\r\n   defect and still emit the word that authorises the release.\r\n\r\nNo process diary. No summary of what was read.\r\n\r\n## When a round comes back all-confirmed\r\n\r\nThe Falsification law governs this: the brief goes on trial, not the subject. Apply it, and if the\r\nclaims turn out to have been descriptive, the successor brief is where the sharper ones go.\r\n\r\n## Reconcile and rule\r\n\r\nFollow `references/reconcile.md`. The obligations that are not delegable:\r\n\r\n- **Reproduce every sharp finding yourself** before acting on it. An auditor's finding is a\r\n  hypothesis until the orchestrator has run it.\r\n- **Build before you pack.** `npm pack` runs no build, so it ships whatever `dist/` is on disk. An\r\n  auditor who packs an artifact to inspect it is reading the last build, not the current source, and\r\n  will report deleted exports as still shipping. Run the package's build first, and say in the brief\r\n  that the tarball was built from the commit under audit.\r\n- **Check the subject before acting on a finding, not only the reasoning.** A brief that names attack\r\n  vectors teaches the lane those vectors matter, and a lane can hand back the brief's own questions as\r\n  the subject's claims - demanding coverage for a property the subject never documented. Grep the\r\n  subject for the claim the finding rests on. Where it is not there, the finding is against the brief.\r\n- **A disagreement between auditors is rarely a tie to average.** It is usually correct answers\r\n  to different questions. Find the question each one answered.\r\n- **Bound every finding**: state what is _not_ broken, and why the adjacent behaviour that looks the\r\n  same is correct. An audit that reports everything is as useless as one that reports nothing.\r\n- **Bound the fix before briefing it.** Establish what over-correcting would break, and include that\r\n  in the fix brief as a constraint.\r\n- Drop, on the record, any finding neither auditor can substantiate.\r\n\r\n## Accept, or run it again\r\n\r\nThe threshold is **a `PASS` terminal line on a brief whose claims cover what the subject owns** -\r\nevery numbered claim `CONFIRMED` on evidence, nothing `UNRESOLVED`, nothing `NOT-EVIDENCED`, no\r\nsubstantiated finding beside them. Never green gates, which prove only that a suite ran.\r\n\r\nA round that finds something is a success, not a delay. The alternative is a consumer finding it\r\nafter publication, when the version number is already spent. But an unsubstantiated attack is not a\r\nfinding, and the supply of imaginable ones never runs out: a round is not re-run because an auditor\r\ncan still think of one. A substantiated finding against something no claim names is real and forces\r\n`FAIL`; an unsubstantiated one is a claim for the successor brief. The escalation law in\r\n`.claude/rules/quality.md` governs a subject that keeps producing findings at the same seam - after\r\nenough of them the ruling owed is on the design, not on the next defect.\r\n\r\nWhen a fix round follows, its auditor must be an engine that did not write it, and the next round's\r\nbrief is the successor of this one.\r\n# Production hardening\r\n\r\n## Build the risk and seam matrix\r\n\r\nList every boundary where assumptions can fail:\r\n\r\n- public inputs and option combinations;\r\n- empty, missing, minimum, maximum, overflow, `NaN`, infinity, and signed-zero values;\r\n- invalid state transitions and repeated lifecycle operations;\r\n- partial work, abort, timeout, retry, pause/resume, stop/destroy, and cleanup;\r\n- concurrency, reentrancy, ordering, idempotency, fairness, and backpressure;\r\n- filesystem, process, socket, protocol, browser, model, and network boundaries;\r\n- hostile strings, paths, headers, frames, JSON, prototypes, getters, and proxies;\r\n- resource ownership, leaks, teardown, and pressure;\r\n- environment isolation and unsupported-host behavior;\r\n- serialization, restore, public exports, and consumer ergonomics.\r\n\r\nFor each applicable seam, state the invariant, failure mode, observable result, and proving test. Omit irrelevant generic cases rather than creating meaningless tests.\r\n\r\n## Use real implementations\r\n\r\nTest through public behavior with:\r\n\r\n| Seam                                    | The real thing it must be driven with                                    |\r\n| --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ |\r\n| Package behavior                        | Real package classes and composed managers                               |\r\n| Filesystem                              | Temporary directories and files                                          |\r\n| Network protocol                        | Actual local sockets and protocol-faithful fixture servers               |\r\n| Browser                                 | An actual Chromium-family browser when the browser is the system         |\r\n| Declared dependency                     | The actual installed dependency                                          |\r\n| External service or model               | The real service in its dedicated project when its behavior is the claim |\r\n| Third-party client or protocol consumer | One representative real foreign client, driven end to end                |\r\n\r\nA third-party seam is the one case where the package's own tests cannot close the claim:\r\nprotocol tests prove the protocol, and only a real client of that class proves the\r\nintegration. Drive it end to end, record the exact commands, and record what that client\r\ncould NOT reach as an honest limit rather than an untested assumption.\r\n\r\nNever use mocks, behavioral fakes, module replacement, or framework spies. Use recorders for callbacks and customizable data factories/stubs for inert shapes.\r\n\r\n## Design live-service tests\r\n\r\nKeep live tests in a dedicated project with explicit readiness, setup, assertions when startup is material.\r\n- Use the smallest prompt/request/context that proves one behavior.\r\n- Make assertions semantic and bounded, not dependent on exact prose.\r\n- For model campaign.\r\n\r\n## Challenge lifecycle and pressure\r\n\r\nExercise:\r\n\r\n- repeated start/stop/pause/resume/abort/destroy calls;\r\n- operations during transitions and after teardown;\r\n- concurrent managers/entities and interleaved completion;\r\n- late events, listener errors, rejection paths, and cleanup after partial setup;\r\n- append/change/remove behavior while work is active handles, leaked processes, sessions, pages, files, or listeners.\r\n\r\nUse bounded pressure tests that expose coordination defects without making the suite gratuitously slow.\r\n\r\n## Audit security
