DEFAULT_RELAY_LIMIT, request.signal)\r\n\t\tif (!read.complete) return new Response(undefined, { status: OVERSIZED_RELAY_STATUS })\r\n\t\tconst parsed = parseJSONAs(read.text, providerRequestContract.is)\r\n\t\tif (parsed === undefined) return new Response(undefined, { status: INVALID_RELAY_STATUS })\r\n\t\treturn new RelayStream({ provider, request: parsed, signal: request.signal }).response\r\n\t}\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates a provider that carries calls through a relay endpoint.\r\n *\r\n * @param options - The endpoint, parser factory, and HTTP call configuration\r\n * @returns The concrete relay provider\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * const provider = createRelayProvider({ url: 'https://relay.example/', parser: createParser })\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createRelayProvider(options: RelayProviderOptions): RelayProvider {\r\n\treturn new RelayProvider(options)\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates a conversation - a {@link ConversationInterface} grouping messages above a flat\r\n * message store it owns directly, with compaction into summarized sections, a regenerated\r\n * rollup `summary`, on-demand `rehydrate`, and substring `search`, driven by a\r\n * provider-agnostic {@link ConversationSummaryHandler} seam.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * Append turns through the conversation's own `add` (the live tail it owns); `view()` is the model input\r\n * (each section as a summary message, then the live tail). `compact()` folds the older live\r\n * messages into a summarized {@link Section} and regenerates the rollup - it requires\r\n * a `summarize` (omitted ? `compact()` throws a `ConversationError`); `keep` retains a recent\r\n * tail (default `DEFAULT_CONVERSATION_KEEP` - fold all). `rehydrate(id)` / `search(query)` read\r\n * the retained originals. Observable (`emitter` - `compact` / `summary` / `rehydrate`), wired\r\n * through the reserved `on` option; the emitter isolates a listener throw and routes it to\r\n * its `error` handler (the `error` option), so it can never corrupt a compaction.\r\n *\r\n * @param options - Optional `id` / `on` hooks + the `summarize` seam + `keep` (see {@link ConversationOptions})\r\n * @returns A working {@link ConversationInterface}\r\n *\r\n * @example Conversations & compaction\r\n * ```ts\r\n * import { createConversation } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n * import type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n *\r\n * declare const provider: ProviderInterface // any concrete implementation supplied by the host app\r\n * // The summarizer seam - built from the provider by the runtime; core stays provider-agnostic.\r\n * // Append the instruction as the FINAL user turn: a chat model emits nothing when the prompt\r\n * // ends on an assistant turn, so a leading-system instruction is unreliable.\r\n * const conversation = createConversation({\r\n * \tsummarize: async (messages) =>\r\n * \t\t(\r\n * \t\t\tawait provider.generate(\r\n * \t\t\t\t[\r\n * \t\t\t\t\t...messages,\r\n * \t\t\t\t\t{ id: 's', role: 'user', content: 'Summarize the conversation so far concisely.' },\r\n * \t\t\t\t],\r\n * \t\t\t\tAbortSignal.timeout(30_000),\r\n * \t\t\t)\r\n * \t\t).content,\r\n * \tkeep: 2, // retain the two most recent turns verbatim on each compaction\r\n * })\r\n * conversation.add([\r\n * \t{ role: 'user', content: 'My name is Ada.' },\r\n * \t{ role: 'assistant', content: 'Nice to meet you, Ada.' },\r\n * \t{ role: 'user', content: 'What did I say my name was?' },\r\n * ])\r\n *\r\n * const section = await conversation.compact() // folds the older turns \u001a a summarized section\r\n * conversation.view() // [<section summary message>, ...the retained recent tail] - the model input\r\n * conversation.summary // the regenerated rollup (a summary-of-summaries over all sections)\r\n * conversation.search('ada') // case-insensitive across sections' originals + the live tail\r\n * section && conversation.rehydrate(section.id) // the section's full original messages (a pure read)\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createConversation(options?: ConversationOptions): ConversationInterface {\r\n\treturn new Conversation(options)\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates a conversation registry - a {@link ConversationManagerInterface} holding\r\n * {@link ConversationInterface}s keyed by their `id`, in insertion order, with an active pointer:\r\n * the id-keyed store over the conversation layer plus the `active` / `switch` seam the context\r\n * renders. `add` auto-activates the first conversation and flows the registry's default\r\n * `summarize` / `keep` into every conversation it creates.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * Starts empty; `add(input?)` mints a {@link ConversationInterface} (its `id` from the input\r\n * or a random UUID), flowing the manager's default `summarize` / `keep` in unless the input\r\n * overrides them, and stores it (an already-present `id` overwrites - last write wins) - and\r\n * auto-activates the first one (a registry with conversations always has one `active`); a later\r\n * `add` leaves `active` unchanged. `switch(id)` re-points `active` (an unknown `id` returns\r\n * `undefined`, leaving `active` unchanged - lenient, never throws); `conversation(id)` /\r\n * `conversations()` look up; `remove` (one or a batch) reports `true` only when every supplied id\r\n * was removed and clears `active` if it was a removed one; `clear` empties it and clears `active`.\r\n * Event-free\r\n * (each conversation owns its own observable `emitter`). A conversation created with neither a\r\n * manager default nor a per-`add` `summarize` cannot `compact` (it throws a `ConversationError`).\r\n *\r\n * @param options - Optional default `summarize` / `keep` (see {@link ConversationManagerOptions})\r\n * @returns An empty {@link ConversationManagerInterface}\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * import { createConversationManager } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n *\r\n * const conversations = createConversationManager({ summarize: async (m) => `recap of ${m.length}` })\r\n * const chat = conversations.add() // auto-activates - conversations.active === chat\r\n * chat.add({ role: 'user', content: 'Hello' })\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createConversationManager(\r\n\toptions?: ConversationManagerOptions,\r\n): ConversationManagerInterface {\r\n\treturn new ConversationManager(options)\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates the in-memory conversation store - a {@link ConversationStoreInterface} backed by a\r\n * process-lifetime `Map` of {@link import('./types.js').ConversationSnapshot}s keyed by conversation\r\n * id, the default backing for the durable {@link ConversationManagerInterface.open} /\r\n * {@link ConversationManagerInterface.save} seam. The exact twin of\r\n * {@link import('@orkestrel/workspace').createMemoryWorkspaceStore}.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * A plain `Map` (the snapshot is already pure JSON, so no encoding is needed for the memory tier),\r\n * the structural twin of {@link import('@orkestrel/workspace').createMemoryWorkspaceStore}.\r\n * `get` / `set` / `delete` are async (the\r\n * same shape a durable backend fits); unlike a session store there is no idle-TTL / eviction - a\r\n * persisted conversation lives until an explicit `delete`. Its driver-pluggable twin is\r\n * {@link createDatabaseConversationStore} (the snapshot as one opaque JSON column over a `databases`\r\n * table) - for a durable store pass it a JSON / SQLite / IndexedDB driver, and it swaps in without\r\n * touching the manager or the conversation. Hydration stays a manager concern: read a snapshot back\r\n * and rebuild the live conversation through the `snapshot` option (re-supplying the live\r\n * `summarize` / `keep`).\r\n *\r\n * @returns A memory-backed {@link ConversationStoreInterface}\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * import { createConversationManager, createMemoryConversationStore } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n *\r\n * const store = createMemoryConversationStore()\r\n * const manager = createConversationManager({ store })\r\n * const conversation = manager.add()\r\n * conversation.add({ role: 'user', content: 'hello' })\r\n * await manager.save(conversation.id)            // persist the conversation\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createMemoryConversationStore(): ConversationStoreInterface {\r\n\treturn new MemoryConversationStore()\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates a {@link DatabaseConversationStore} over any {@link DriverInterface}, defaulting to\r\n * `createMemoryDriver()` - the durable, driver-pluggable backing for the conversation persistence\r\n * seam, holding each snapshot as one opaque JSON column and standing as the opt-in twin of\r\n * {@link createMemoryConversationStore}. The exact twin of\r\n * {@link import('@orkestrel/workspace').createDatabaseWorkspaceStore}.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * Builds a one-table database (`conversations`, keyed by `id`) over the supplied driver, the snapshot\r\n * held as one opaque JSON column - the column map is `{ id; snapshot }` where `snapshot` is a\r\n * `rawShape` (a JSON blob), exactly as\r\n * {@link import('@orkestrel/workspace').createDatabaseWorkspaceStore} stores its snapshot. The\r\n * snapshot is already a complete, self-contained, pure-JSON payload, so storing it whole is lossless\r\n * and keeps the row type flat (the column reads back as `unknown`, narrowed on `get` by\r\n * {@link import('./validators.js').isConversationSnapshot}). The `driver` defaults to\r\n * {@link createMemoryDriver}, so the store also works in memory out of the box; pass a server\r\n * `createJSONDriver` / `createSQLiteDriver` (or a browser IndexedDB driver) for a persistent one -\r\n * the durability is the driver's job, the store engine is shared. It swaps in behind\r\n * {@link ConversationStoreInterface} without touching the manager or the conversation.\r\n *\r\n * @param driver - The storage backend the snapshots persist to (defaults to {@link createMemoryDriver})\r\n * @returns A {@link ConversationStoreInterface} over the driver\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * import { createConversationManager, createDatabaseConversationStore } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n * import { createMemoryDriver } from '@orkestrel/database'\r\n *\r\n * const store = createDatabaseConversationStore(createMemoryDriver()) // a durable driver swaps in here\r\n * const manager = createConversationManager({ store })\r\n * const conversation = manager.add()\r\n * conversation.add({ role: 'user', content: 'hello' })\r\n * await manager.save(conversation.id)            // persist the conversation (one JSON column)\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createDatabaseConversationStore(\r\n\tdriver: DriverInterface = createMemoryDriver(),\r\n): ConversationStoreInterface {\r\n\t// The snapshot is stored as one opaque JSON column (`rawShape`), so the row infers flat -\r\n\t// `{ id: string; snapshot: unknown }` = `ConversationSnapshotRow` - and the sections/messages\r\n\t// snapshot shape never forces a contract `Infer`.\r\n\tconst columns = { id: stringShape(), snapshot: rawShape({}) }\r\n\tconst database = createDatabase({ driver, tables: { conversations: columns } })\r\n\tconst table: TableInterface<ConversationSnapshotRow> = database.table('conversations')\r\n\treturn new DatabaseConversationStore(table)\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates an instruction - an immutable {@link InstructionInterface} (a named directive)\r\n * from its `name` / `content` and optional `priority`, the `id` minted at construction.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * Only `name` / `content` are required; `priority` orders the instruction in an\r\n * {@link InstructionManagerInterface}'s rendered list (higher first) and defaults to `0`.\r\n * Stored immutable - never mutated after creation.\r\n *\r\n * @param input - `name` / `content` (required) and an optional `priority` (see\r\n *   {@link InstructionInput})\r\n * @returns A working {@link InstructionInterface}\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * import { createInstruction } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n *\r\n * const instruction = createInstruction({ name: 'tone', content: 'Be concise.', priority: 5 })\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createInstruction(input: InstructionInput): InstructionInterface {\r\n\treturn new Instruction(input)\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates an instruction registry - an {@link InstructionManagerInterface} holding\r\n * immutable instructions keyed by `name`, listed by descending `priority`.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * Starts empty; `add` (one or a batch) mints each `id` and overwrites a same-name\r\n * instruction (last write wins); `instructions()` lists them sorted by descending\r\n * `priority` (stable for ties); `open` / `render` are the build contract a richer\r\n * context renders an instructions block with; `remove` (one or a batch) reports `true` only\r\n * when every supplied name was removed; `clear` empties it. Carries an observable `emitter`\r\n * ({@link import('./types.js').InstructionManagerEventMap}) wired through the reserved `on`\r\n * option; the emitter isolates a listener throw and routes it to its `error` handler\r\n * (the `error` option), so it can never corrupt a mutation. An optional `format`\r\n * override is the manager-options level of the `AgentContext` build cascade (consulted by\r\n * `open` / `render`, beating the provider default + built-in; a per-item\r\n * `InstructionInput.override` still beats it).\r\n *\r\n * @param options - Optional `on` hooks + a `format` override (see {@link InstructionManagerOptions})\r\n * @returns An empty {@link InstructionManagerInterface}\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * import { createInstructionManager } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n *\r\n * const instructions = createInstructionManager()\r\n * instructions.add({ name: 'tone', content: 'Be concise.', priority: 5 })\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createInstructionManager(\r\n\toptions?: InstructionManagerOptions,\r\n): InstructionManagerInterface {\r\n\treturn new InstructionManager(options)\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates a named scope - an immutable {@link ScopeInterface} from its `name` and its\r\n * per-category allow-lists, the `id` minted at construction.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * Each list is three-way: `undefined` ? no constraint on that category (all pass), `[]` ?\r\n * none pass, a non-empty list ? only the listed keys pass. `narrow(config)` composes a\r\n * tighter child by set-intersection (an `undefined` side imposing no constraint). Stored\r\n * immutable - never mutated after creation (`narrow` returns a new scope).\r\n *\r\n * @param input - `name` (required) and the optional `instructions` / `tools` / `files`\r\n *   allow-lists (see {@link ScopeInput})\r\n * @returns A working {@link ScopeInterface}\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * import { createScope } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n *\r\n * const reader = createScope({ name: 'reader', tools: ['search', 'read'] })\r\n * reader.narrow({ tools: ['read', 'write'] }).tools // ['read'] - intersection tightens\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createScope(input: ScopeInput): ScopeInterface {\r\n\treturn new Scope(input)\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates a scope registry - a {@link ScopeManagerInterface} holding immutable scopes keyed\r\n * by their minted `id`, in insertion order.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * Starts empty; `create` mints each scope's `id` and stores it (keyed by `id`, so it\r\n * always adds - two scopes may share a `name`); `scopes()` lists them in insertion order;\r\n * `remove` (one or a batch) reports `true` only when every supplied id was removed; `clear`\r\n * empties it. Carries an observable `emitter`\r\n * ({@link import('./types.js').ScopeManagerEventMap}) wired through the reserved `on`\r\n * option; the emitter isolates a listener throw and routes it to its `error` handler\r\n * (the `error` option), so it can never corrupt a mutation.\r\n *\r\n * @param options - Optional `on` hooks (see {@link ScopeManagerOptions})\r\n * @returns An empty {@link ScopeManagerInterface}\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * import { createScopeManager } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n *\r\n * const scopes = createScopeManager()\r\n * const reader = scopes.create({ name: 'reader', tools: ['search'] })\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createScopeManager(options?: ScopeManagerOptions): ScopeManagerInterface {\r\n\treturn new ScopeManager(options)\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates a richer turn context - an {@link AgentContextInterface} assembling a provider request\r\n * from the optional system prompt, the instruction registry, the workspace registry (the only\r\n * document channel), the conversation registry that is its `messages` source, the tool registry,\r\n * and the active scope, which `build()` folds into the next turn's input.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * `system` is the optional system prompt; `tools` / `instructions` / `workspaces` are pre-built\r\n * managers to reuse (empty ones are created when omitted, so `context.workspaces` is always\r\n * present); `scope` is the initial active filter (`undefined` ? no filtering, changeable afterwards\r\n * through `context.apply(...)`). The `messages` store is always fresh. `build()` folds the scoped\r\n * instructions - plus the active workspace's scope-filtered text files (fenced) - into one leading\r\n * `system` message and appends the scoped conversation (attaching the active workspace's\r\n * scope-filtered image files' `base64` payload to the last user message), built fresh each call; the active\r\n * workspace is the sole document/image context. Tools are advertised structurally (through\r\n * `tools.definitions()`, scope-filtered by the loop), never serialized into the prompt.\r\n *\r\n * @param options - Optional `system` / `tools` / `instructions` / `workspaces` / `scope`\r\n *   (see {@link AgentContextOptions})\r\n * @returns A working {@link AgentContextInterface}\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * import { createAgentContext } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n *\r\n * const context = createAgentContext({ system: 'You are concise.' })\r\n * context.instructions.add({ name: 'tone', content: 'Be terse.' })\r\n * context.messages.add({ role: 'user', content: 'Hi' })\r\n * context.build() // [{ role: 'system', content: 'You are concise.\\n\\n## Instructions\\n\\nBe terse.' }, { role: 'user', content: 'Hi' }]\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createAgentContext(options?: AgentContextOptions): AgentContextInterface {\r\n\treturn new AgentContext(options)\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates an agent loop - an {@link AgentInterface} composing a\r\n * {@link ProviderInterface}, its {@link AgentContextInterface}, and a tool registry\r\n * into a bounded context \u001a provider \u001a tools \u001a repeat turn, exposed as a one-shot\r\n * `generate` and a live `stream`.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * One private loop drives the turn; `generate` drains the same stream `stream`\r\n * exposes, so they can never diverge. Each turn is bounded by one cancel folded from\r\n * `signal` + `timeout` + `budget` (through `AbortSignal.any`) - any trip (or `abort()`)\r\n * commits a partial result (the stream's `result` resolves on a cancel, rejects only\r\n * on a genuine provider / tool error). The `scheduler` paces between turns; tool\r\n * iteration is capped at `limit` (default `DEFAULT_AGENT_LIMIT`). Tools are advertised\r\n * structurally through `context.tools.definitions()`. Two observation surfaces: the\r\n * {@link AgentChunk} stream (pull - per-token content) and a typed `emitter` (push -\r\n * lifecycle + `usage` / `tool` / `deny` for fire-and-forget observers).\r\n *\r\n * @param provider - The {@link ProviderInterface} the loop drives each turn\r\n * @param options - Optional `system` / `tools` / `limit` / `timeout` / `budget` /\r\n *   `scheduler` / `signal` (see {@link AgentOptions})\r\n * @returns A working {@link AgentInterface}\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * import type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n * import { createAgent } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n * import { createTokenBudget } from '@orkestrel/budget'\r\n *\r\n * declare const provider: ProviderInterface // any concrete implementation supplied by the host app\r\n * const agent = createAgent(provider, {\r\n * \tsystem: 'You are concise.',\r\n * \tbudget: createTokenBudget({ max: 50_000, scope: 'total' }),\r\n * })\r\n * agent.context.messages.add({ role: 'user', content: 'Say hi.' })\r\n *\r\n * const stream = agent.stream()\r\n * for await (const chunk of stream.events) {\r\n * \tif (chunk.category === 'token') process.stdout.write(chunk.content)\r\n * }\r\n * const result = await stream.result // { content, usage?, partial }\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createAgent(provider: ProviderInterface, options?: AgentOptions): AgentInterface {\r\n\treturn new Agent(provider, options)\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates a fresh stream-stateful `<think>` separator - a {@link ThinkSplitterInterface} that\r\n * splits a thinking model's in-content `<think>.</think>` reasoning spans away from the answer,\r\n * delta by delta, so a provider yields clean content alone and surfaces the accumulated\r\n * reasoning as {@link import('./types.js').ProviderResult.thinking}. One splitter serves one\r\n * stream.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * Feed each raw wire delta through `split(delta)` (it returns the clean content to\r\n * surface - possibly `''` mid-think) and settle the stream end with `flush()` (a held\r\n * partial open tag that never completed returns as final content; an unclosed think\r\n * span lands on `thinking`). Tags split across deltas are held back until\r\n * disambiguated, multiple spans accumulate in order, and a nested-looking `<think>`\r\n * inside an open span is thinking text. One splitter serves one stream - create\r\n * a fresh one per provider call.\r\n *\r\n * @returns A fresh {@link ThinkSplitterInterface} (state empty, outside any span)\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * import { createThinkSplitter } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n *\r\n * const splitter = createThinkSplitter()\r\n * const clean = splitter.split('<think>plan the answer</think>Here it is.')\r\n * clean // 'Here it is.'\r\n * splitter.thinking // 'plan the answer'\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createThinkSplitter(): ThinkSplitterInterface {\r\n\treturn new ThinkSplitter()\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates an empty unbounded async channel - a {@link ChannelInterface} a producer writes\r\n * values into (`push`) and ends (`close` / `fail`) regardless of consumption, while a\r\n * consumer reads them back live through `drain`.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * Write and read are decoupled, so the producer never waits for a consumer: an agent's eager\r\n * pump writes each chunk into one, which is why a run's `result` settles whether or not the\r\n * live events are drained. A value pushed at an already-parked consumer is delivered, buffered\r\n * values are yielded before the end is reported, and the first failure wins.\r\n *\r\n * @typeParam T - The value type the channel carries\r\n * @returns A fresh, empty {@link ChannelInterface}\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * import { createChannel } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n *\r\n * const channel = createChannel<number>()\r\n * channel.push(1)\r\n * channel.close()\r\n * for await (const value of channel.drain()) {\r\n * \tvalue // 1\r\n * }\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createChannel<T>(): ChannelInterface<T> {\r\n\treturn new Channel<T>()\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates a policy gate - an {@link AuthorityInterface} the agent loop consults before\r\n * each tool call runs, evaluating the ordered rules first-match-wins and falling back\r\n * to the configured default when none match.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * `rules` are evaluated in order - the first whose `match` is true decides (a matched\r\n * rule allows unless its `allowed` is explicitly `false`). When no rule matches, the\r\n * `fallback` decides; it defaults to `{ zone: DEFAULT_AUTHORITY_ZONE, allowed: true }`\r\n * (allow-unmatched - a rules list of denials acts as a denylist). Pass an\r\n * `allowed: false` `fallback` to flip the gate to deny-by-default (an allowlist). Wire\r\n * the result into `createAgent` through `AgentOptions.authority`: a denied call is fed back\r\n * to the model as a denial `ToolResult` (not executed, no budget cost), so the model\r\n * can react. Synchronous - `evaluate` returns the verdict directly.\r\n *\r\n * @param options - Optional `rules` (ordered) and `fallback` (see {@link AuthorityOptions})\r\n * @returns A working {@link AuthorityInterface}\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * import { createAgent, createAuthority } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n *\r\n * // Deny the `delete` tool, allow everything else.\r\n * const authority = createAuthority({\r\n * \trules: [{ match: (c) => c.call.name === 'delete', zone: 'restricted', allowed: false }],\r\n * })\r\n * const agent = createAgent(provider, { tools, authority })\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createAuthority(options?: AuthorityOptions): AuthorityInterface {\r\n\treturn new Authority(options)\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates an agent registry - an {@link AgentRegistryInterface} holding the named pools of\r\n * live, non-serializable pieces (providers, tools, authorities, schedulers) that a\r\n * serializable {@link AgentJobInput}'s names resolve against, and `build`ing a seeded,\r\n * signal-wired {@link AgentInterface} from a job.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * `providers` is required; `tools` / `authorities` / `schedulers` are optional pools.\r\n * The accessors (`provider` / `tool` / `authority` / `scheduler`) throw an\r\n * {@link import('./errors.js').AgentError} carrying `code: 'REGISTRY'` and the message\r\n * `unknown <category>: <name>` on an unregistered name - a misconfigured or crash-restored\r\n * job fails loudly rather than running with a missing dependency. `build(input, signal)`\r\n * resolves the names, rebuilds the token budget from its ceiling, seeds the agent's\r\n * context with the job's messages, and threads `signal` so a queue / runner abort\r\n * propagates. This is the bridge that makes durable, serializable agent jobs runnable.\r\n *\r\n * @param options - The named pools (see {@link AgentRegistryOptions})\r\n * @returns A working {@link AgentRegistryInterface}\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * import { createAgentRegistry } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n * import type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n * import { createTool } from '@orkestrel/tool'\r\n *\r\n * declare const provider: ProviderInterface // any concrete implementation supplied by the host app\r\n * const registry = createAgentRegistry({\r\n * \tproviders: { main: provider },\r\n * \ttools: { add: createTool({ name: 'add', execute: (a) => Number(a.x) + Number(a.y) }) },\r\n * })\r\n * const agent = registry.build({ provider: 'main', messages: [{ role: 'user', content: 'Hi.' }] })\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createAgentRegistry(options: AgentRegistryOptions): AgentRegistryInterface {\r\n\treturn new AgentRegistry(options)\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates a durable, bounded-concurrency agent-job queue - a {@link QueueInterface} over\r\n * serializable {@link AgentJobInput}s that composes `createQueue`: each job is rehydrated\r\n * through the `registry` into a live {@link AgentInterface}, run to its {@link AgentResult},\r\n * and subjected to the partial-as-configurable-failure policy.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * - **Composes the substrate (no new engine).** The handler is the only new logic;\r\n *   bounded `concurrency`, `retries`, the per-attempt `timeout`, and durable persistence\r\n *   through `store` (+ `restore()` after a crash) are all the backing Queue's. `enqueue`\r\n *   returns a per-job promise.\r\n * - **Durable + serializable.** Because `AgentJobInput` is JSON-serializable, a `store`\r\n *   (for example `createMemoryQueueStore` / `createDatabaseQueueStore`) persists outstanding\r\n *   jobs; `restore()` re-enqueues them after a restart and the `registry` rehydrates the\r\n *   live pieces from the names - so a job survives a crash.\r\n * - **Partial policy.** A partial result throws an\r\n *   {@link import('./errors.js').AgentJobError} by default, so a job cancelled by its\r\n *   attempt deadline / a queue abort retries while attempts remain; `partial: true`\r\n *   resolves the partial as success instead.\r\n * - **Cancellation threads through.** The handler passes `context.signal` into\r\n *   `registry.build`, so a queue `abort()` or a per-attempt timeout cancels the in-flight\r\n *   agent (which commits a partial \u001a throws \u001a retries / fails per policy).\r\n *\r\n * @param options - The `registry`, the `partial` policy, and the substrate knobs\r\n *   (`concurrency` / `retries` / `timeout` / `store`) (see {@link AgentQueueOptions})\r\n * @returns A {@link QueueInterface} of {@link AgentJobInput} \u001a {@link AgentResult}\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * import { createAgentQueue, createAgentRegistry } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n * import { createMemoryQueueStore } from '@orkestrel/queue'\r\n *\r\n * const registry = createAgentRegistry({ providers: { main: provider } })\r\n * const store = createMemoryQueueStore(agentJobShape) // survives a restart through restore()\r\n * const queue = createAgentQueue({ registry, concurrency: 2, retries: 1, store })\r\n * const result = await queue.enqueue({ provider: 'main', messages: [{ role: 'user', content: 'ok?' }] })\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createAgentQueue(\r\n\toptions: AgentQueueOptions,\r\n): QueueInterface<AgentJobInput, AgentResult> {\r\n\tconst { registry, partial = false, concurrency, retries, timeout, store } = options\r\n\treturn createQueue<AgentJobInput, AgentResult>({\r\n\t\t...(concurrency === undefined ? {} : { concurrency }),\r\n\t\t...(retries === undefined ? {} : { retries }),\r\n\t\t...(timeout === undefined ? {} : { timeout }),\r\n\t\t...(store === undefined ? {} : { store }),\r\n\t\thandler: handleAgentQueueJob.bind(undefined, registry, partial),\r\n\t})\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates an agent-job runner - a {@link RunnerInterface} over serializable\r\n * {@link AgentJobInput}s that composes `createRunner` (one-shot, ordered, fail-fast), each unit\r\n * rehydrated through the `registry` and subjected to the partial policy. The runner also carries\r\n * sub-agent fan-out: a parent job's handler can `controller.spawn(childJob)`.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * - **Composes the substrate (no new engine).** Bounded `concurrency`, `retries`, the\r\n *   per-attempt `timeout`, ordered results, and fail-fast are all the backing Runner's;\r\n *   the handler adds only rehydration + the partial policy.\r\n * - **Sub-agent fan-out.** Each unit's handler receives a `ControllerInterface` whose\r\n *   `spawn(childJob)` launches a child agent job through the same bounded queue (the\r\n *   child's result joins the run after the declared units, in spawn order). On a bounded\r\n *   runner, fan out and return - do not inline-`await` a spawn from within the handler (a\r\n *   slot-holding handler awaiting its own spawn can deadlock; see `ControllerInterface`).\r\n * - **Partial policy + cancellation.** Same as `createAgentQueue`: a partial result\r\n *   throws by default (the run's fail-fast engages), `partial: true` resolves it; the\r\n *   handler threads `controller.signal` into `registry.build`, so a runner abort / a\r\n *   per-attempt timeout cancels the agent.\r\n *\r\n * @param options - The `registry`, the `partial` policy, and the substrate knobs\r\n *   (`concurrency` / `retries` / `timeout`) (see {@link AgentRunnerOptions})\r\n * @returns A {@link RunnerInterface} of {@link AgentJobInput} \u001a {@link AgentResult}\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * import { createAgentRunner, createAgentRegistry } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n *\r\n * const registry = createAgentRegistry({ providers: { main: provider } })\r\n * const runner = createAgentRunner({ registry, concurrency: 2 })\r\n * // Run two jobs; the first fans out a child sub-agent then returns.\r\n * const child = { provider: 'main', messages: [{ role: 'user', content: 'child' }] }\r\n * const parent = { provider: 'main', messages: [{ role: 'user', content: 'parent' }] }\r\n * const results = await runner.execute([parent, child]) // declared first, then any spawns\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createAgentRunner(\r\n\toptions: AgentRunnerOptions,\r\n): RunnerInterface<AgentJobInput, AgentResult> {\r\n\tconst { registry, partial = false, concurrency, retries, timeout } = options\r\n\treturn createRunner<AgentJobInput, AgentResult>({\r\n\t\t...(concurrency === undefined ? {} : { concurrency }),\r\n\t\t...(retries === undefined ? {} : { retries }),\r\n\t\t...(timeout === undefined ? {} : { timeout }),\r\n\t\thandler: handleAgentRunnerJob.bind(undefined, registry, partial),\r\n\t})\r\n}\r\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_18","type":"command_execution","command":"\"C:\\\\WINDOWS\\\\System32\\\\WindowsPowerShell\\\\v1.0\\\\powershell.exe\" -Command 'Get-Content ../scaffold/.claude/rules/quality.md -Encoding utf8'","aggregated_output":"---\r\npaths:\r\n  - 'src/**/*'\r\n  - 'app/**/*'\r\n  - 'tests/**/*'\r\n  - 'guides/**/*'\r\n  - 'package.json'\r\n  - 'vite.config.ts'\r\n  - 'tsconfig.json'\r\n  - '.agents/skills/**/*'\r\n  - '.claude/skills/**/*'\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Research, hardening, and completion rules\r\n\r\n## Evidence before change\r\n\r\n- Research when the user asks for it, when comparing an upstream, protocol, or legacy implementation, or when current external behavior materially affects design.\r\n- Use current primary sources for external capabilities, and the exact installed declarations and guides for dependencies. Separate verified fact from inference.\r\n- Read authoritative types and named decision-bearing implementation files first-hand. Delegate bulk supporting context, never the owning design decision.\r\n- Treat existing code, tests, `old/`, branches, and copied projects as evidence, not authority.\r\n- Build a capability/defect matrix before a broad API or production-readiness change. Every row ends as implement, repair, retain, or intentionally exclude with evidence.\r\n- Fix that matrix when the change starts. It is the definition of done, and the change closes when every row closes.\r\n- Never end a row as \"hardened further.\" Replace any evaluative phrase with the concrete condition that closes the row.\r\n- Record a finding outside the matrix against the row that owns it, for the next matrix. Do not reopen this one.\r\n\r\n## Probes before arguments\r\n\r\nA question a probe can settle is settled by the probe, whether or not anyone has disputed it. Reasoning decides what to run and what the result means. It does not replace the run.\r\n\r\n- Test your own assumptions before you rely on them. You do not need a disagreement to justify a probe. If you are about to depend on what a function returns, what a config resolves to, what a flag does, or whether a path is even reached, run it and find out.\r\n- Treat a long deliberation about behaviour as the signal to stop and run something. Deliberation that a ten-line probe would have ended is the most expensive habit in this process, and it is invisible because it feels like rigour.\r\n- Verify a belief before stating it, or label it as unverified. An unverified assertion put into context becomes a fact for everything downstream, including other agents, and correcting it later costs more than the check would have.\r\n- Hold a claim you issue to the standard you hold a claim you receive. Nothing downstream re-checks an instruction, so an unverified fact inside one becomes the premise of every task built on it. Auditing every report you receive while exempting every brief you write is the common form of this failure.\r\n- Prefer the check over the chain of reasoning whenever the check is cheap. Reading the installed declaration, running one line, or counting the call sites beats three paragraphs inferring the same thing, and it produces evidence you can hand to someone else.\r\n- Write the probe before the argument gets long. The moment a claim about behaviour is disputed - between engines, between a report and a rule, or in your own head - stop and run it. Get the smallest real input through the real code and read the real output.\r\n- Bound the search before starting it, and put the bound in the brief. Name the benchmark, the population, or the row that ends it. An investigation with no stated stopping condition runs until attention runs out. This includes the case where the honest answer is that the limit is inherent and belongs in documentation rather than in code.\r\n- Treat a negative probe as evidence about the probe until its input is shown to reach the code under test. A pass proves nothing if the vector never arrived. Instrument the path, assert an observable side effect, or drive it through a door you can see.\r\n- When a report names a defect and your reproduction comes back clean, assume first that your vector was weaker than theirs, and go get their exact vector. Treating a failed reproduction as a disproof is the most common way a true finding is lost.\r\n- A reported vector the compiler rejects refutes the VECTOR, never the finding. Where a claim asserts reachability through a typed API, compile the exact vector under the project's own settings; re-derive one the types admit before dropping the finding, and record which vector was actually tested.\r\n- Reproduce a reported defect's cause before instructing a fix from it. A real symptom can carry a wrong diagnosis, and a fix aimed at the stated cause edits the wrong file while the defect survives.\r\n- Do not read a result into a tool's failure to find your probe. \"No tests found\", an empty match, a skipped file, a runner that resolved nothing - these report on the harness, not the subject. Confirm the probe was collected and executed first.\r\n- Prefer an observation over a derivation, including your own. When a measurement and an argument disagree, the argument is wrong until the measurement is shown to be broken.\r\n- Diagnose from the artifact the work produces - the file, the count, the exit code, the timestamp on what changed - never from a wrapper or a proxy signal that merely correlates with progress. Read a gate bare: a pipeline stage after it (`| tail`, `| grep`) reports the stage's exit status and hides the gate's failing lines.\r\n- Verify a comment or an agent's report against the call sites before relying on it. A code comment is not evidence. When lanes disagree about whether a path is live, count the callers rather than weighing the prose.\r\n- What a round proves is what it ran. A conclusion carried from one door to another is a hypothesis at the second door. Re-run it there.\r\n\r\n## Falsification\r\n\r\nA review that reads a diff finds what the diff shows. A review that tries to break named claims finds what the diff hides. Code that has passed diff review several times can still carry a defect nobody has tried to trigger.\r\n\r\n### Writing the claims\r\n\r\n- State the subject as a numbered list of the claims the work makes, never as \"review this diff.\" Each claim is falsifiable: a property some concrete input, state, or interleaving could show false.\r\n- Instruct the auditor to attempt refutation, not confirmation. A claim it cannot break is CONFIRMED with the evidence that convinced it. A claim it breaks is BROKEN with the exact failing input, state, or interleaving, plus the smallest correct fix.\r\n- Derive claims from what the change asserts under adverse conditions: cancellation, restart, concurrency, partial failure, hostile input, resource exhaustion, and the orderings a happy path never reaches.\r\n- Read the installed declaration or implementation of every substrate a claim depends on. A claim about `stop()` is unfalsifiable until you know what `stop()` does when the status is not the one the caller assumed.\r\n\r\n### Instruments\r\n\r\n- An instrument is not evidence until it has failed. Pair every probe, comparison, or matrix with a negative control that must report failure, run under the same conditions. An identity check whose control reports \"same\" has measured nothing.\r\n- When a question about a TypeScript edit can supply a workspace project, a case of workspace files with a test, and a negative control naming its files, its test, the stage it must fail at, and why, call the `prove` tool the `probe` MCP server registers before relying on the answer. When the question supplies no project, no case, or no control, follow `.claude/rules/tests.md` \u0015 Probes and report the fallback instrument's own control and coverage.\r\n- When no `probe` server is registered in the session, register one outside the repository, in the harness's own local or user MCP scope - in Claude Code, `claude mcp add` outside project scope - naming the installed `node_modules/@orkestrel/probe/dist/bin/main.js` entry, and start it in the repository whose projects the question names, because the server fixes its workspace from its own working directory. The registration cannot live in the tree: a scaffold target holds no `.mcp.json` file, that path is instruction canon, and a copy at it reports as foreign drift on every `scaffold audit` run. Where the harness registers no server at all, treat the question as supplying no project and take the preceding rule's fallback.\r\n- Quote the closing line of the `prove` answer verbatim in every report, brief, and audit verdict that rests on the claim: the `receipt probe:<digest>:.` line when the case ran clean and the control broke exactly where the claim declared it would, and the `no receipt` line otherwise. A `no receipt` line leaves the claim unproved - report it with the stage that refused.\r\n- Read a receipt as evidence about its claim, never as a gate result. The gate chain still runs, and `verifier` still owns its result.\r\n- Draw the negative control from outside the population the instrument covers. Name the instrument's membership rule first, then pick a control that rule excludes. A control sampled from constructs the instrument already handles proves only that it discriminates among those constructs, and says nothing about the class it silently cannot reach.\r\n- State an instrument's coverage beside its result. A conclusion inherits the instrument's scope, not the question's. An unstated coverage claim is read as complete, and it never is. A search proves something about the paths it walked, so name them.\r\n- Match the instrument to the question. A text search reports on text, so a claim about declarations, call sites, or structure needs the compiler or a parser instead. A pattern written for one spelling of a construct reports on that spelling alone. A path check answers relative to the directory it runs from, so resolve the inputs against their own base before reading a miss as a finding.\r\n- Name the rival reading the instrument must exclude, and show it reports differently under that reading. Give independent measurements independent state: one counter shared across members reports read order and per-member read count identically, so a result consistent with both measured neither.\r\n- Report a question unanswered rather than answering it with a weaker instrument. A fallback that measures something adjacent returns a confident wrong answer, and nothing downstream can tell that answer from the real one - searching commit messages for a release when the question is where a version changed will match some release, but not the one asked about. Name the substitute and what it actually measures, or say the question is open.\r\n- State what the controls established and what they did not. An instrument certified only from the inside is trusted exactly where it has never been tested.\r\n- Treat a gap between what an instrument says it checks and what it actually matches as a defect in the instrument, not as a documented limit. A recorded blind spot buys trust only when everything outside it is genuinely covered.\r\n- Measure the product, not the harness. A recorded baseline that counts something about its own fixture is not evidence about the shipped surface, however often a guide quotes it.\r\n- Baseline a published-artifact claim against the published artifact. \"Did my change move the surface\" and \"does this release differ from the last one\" are different questions, and a diff against your own starting point answers only the first. A toolchain that re-emits declarations moves the artifact without any source edit, so every writer can correctly report an unmoved surface while the package's published contract has changed. Fetch what consumers actually have - the tarball, the deployed asset - and compare against that.\r\n- Prove a module cycle by loading the built artifact, not by a green suite. Tests import through the source graph and a bundler resolves it differently, so a cycle that is fatal at module-init in the shipped form can stay invisible under every test. Import each published entry point and read an export from it.\r\n- Adopt an instrument that settled a claim as a test before accepting the work it settled. The probe that proved a fix, carrying the control that proved the probe, is that fix's regression guard. A verification that runs once is a rehearsal, not a gate.\r\n\r\n### Rounds and verdicts\r\n\r\n- Treat an all-confirmed round as a legitimate result, and put the brief on trial rather than the subject. Re-read the claims and ask whether any could have been falsified by evidence the round actually had. If none could, the claims were descriptive and the round proved nothing - sharpen them and re-run. If they could have been and were not, the pass stands.\r\n- Name the claims you could not break either way, so the next round knows what has already been attacked.\r\n- Never tell an auditor that a clean round means it did not try. That instructs it to manufacture a finding, and a manufactured finding costs a fix unit, an argument, and the credibility of the true findings beside it.\r\n- Treat a repaired claim as a new claim, not a settled one. Re-ask it at every entry point that reaches the same rule, not only the door the defect arrived through. The engine that wrote the fix is least able to see this, because re-verifying where the fix is feels like verifying the fix.\r\n- A fix that adopts the auditor's prescription verbatim may close with a mutation probe in place of a fresh audit round: disable the load-bearing line, watch the adopted pin fail, restore it, and commit the pin as the regression guard. A fix that departs from the prescription gets the cross-engine round.\r\n- Let reachability bound the fix. A defect reachable through the package's own shipped code or a documented extension seam falsifies its claim and is repaired now.\r\n- Document the obligation instead when a defect is reachable only through a hypothetical foreign implementation of a contract this package publishes. State it on the interface that owns it and prove the documentation. Do not build coordination machinery against a requirement nobody wrote down. Attacks are unlimited; reachable ones are not, and only the reachable set is a work list.\r\n- **Three rounds at one seam is the budget, and reaching it switches the search strategy rather than stopping the work.** Repeated rounds against one seam are evidence about the design, not evidence of diligence. At the third round, name what the audit is trying to accomplish, then pick the successor strategy from what the recurrence shows.\r\n- Recurrence with a direction - each fix relocates the class along one stream, or the evidence points up a dependency chain - ends the depth search. Run one breadth round that probes the stream's stations in parallel to locate the source, in the shape `.agents/orchestration.md` \u0015 Context and decomposition prescribes, then plan the downstream work from the source and size it by the sweep's map.\r\n- Recurrence with no direction makes the seam itself the question. The next unit is a ruling - on the threat model, the mechanism, or the boundary - taken with the same adversarial pass a design gets, not a fourth repair.\r\n- A subject that reprices itself on every edit - a count, a census, a total over prose - has no closing condition and is not a seam. Drop the claim, or recast it as the property the tally stood in for.\r\n- Write the round count down in the capability/defect matrix row that owns the seam, when the seam opens, so it is a fact rather than a feeling. A seam that has consumed more rounds than the rest of the matrix combined has already answered the question.\r\n- State the ruling that ends a seam as the invariant the code will obey, the constraint bounding it against over-correction, and the interface where a consumer meets the obligation. A ruling that names only the defect it replaces produces the opposite defect next round.\r\n- Give every behavioural audit the means to run its attacks. An auditor that cannot execute cannot falsify a behavioural claim: it returns derivations, and a derivation reads exactly like a verdict while being a different thing - it will confirm a claim one probe would break. Treat a report with no executed evidence as a review of the source, and label it as such.\r\n\r\n## Ecosystem reuse\r\n\r\nThe root laws on inspecting declared `@orkestrel/*` capabilities, reusing a matching primitive, and updating every consumer without shims bind here without restatement. This file adds the judgment calls:\r\n\r\n- Prove the semantic difference before keeping a local variant. Similar names are not evidence of different behavior.\r\n- Treat downstream friction as valid evidence of a reusable upstream defect, not as automatic proof. Fix the lowest package that owns the general mechanism and keep product policy downstream.\r\n- Never re-export a dependency's symbol to soften a consumer's import.\r\n\r\n## Production hardening\r\n\r\n- Translate \"enterprise-grade\" or \"production-ready\" into an explicit risk and seam matrix covering applicable inputs, states, failures, cleanup, cancellation, concurrency, resource ownership, hostile boundaries, environment isolation, serialization and restore, and package consumption.\r\n- Grade that matrix on coverage, not optimization: whether every applicable seam exists, works, and stays proven. Do not grade on how many further interleavings can be invented against the one seam already proven. A surface polished past its row buys less than the next uncovered seam would have.\r\n- Test observable invariants at each applicable seam with real implementations.\r\n- Use dedicated real-service projects for external model or service behavior. Require readiness and tune each request to the smallest proof that still exercises the claim.\r\n- Audit test discovery, counts, skipped and todo tests, cleanup, and assertion adequacy. Passing discovered tests alone is insufficient.\r\n- Inspect public exports, declarations, supported runtime targets, and generated outputs.\r\n- Treat a claim that a surface works with an external client as unproven until one representative real client of that class has driven it end to end. Protocol tests prove the protocol, not the integration.\r\n- Add an independent adversarial review for security, destructive paths, concurrency, protocols, or untrusted external input.\r\n\r\n## Completion\r\n\r\nThe root completion law - finish every in-scope capability now, leave no TODO, deferral, or hidden follow-up, and run the applicable repository skill for comprehensive work - binds here without restatement. Stopping there is correct, and the next scope is the deliverable. A further pass over the same surface requires a new instruction from the user, not an auditor's remaining appetite. Fully specified mechanical writing goes\r\n  to `builder` or `application` on the harness's cheap native tier.\r\n- `verifier` runs commands and reports exit codes, so it stays on the native tier too.\r\n\r\n## Orchestrator and executor\r\n\r\n- The top-level agent is the **Orchestrator**. It owns the goal, plan, decisions, cross-unit\r\n  state, integration, and final acceptance.\r\n- A dispatched subagent is an **Executor**. It performs its bounded assignment directly, spawns\r\n  nothing, and returns the required distillate.\r\n- Work directly on a typo, a one-line fix, or a single lookup. Orchestrate when isolation,\r\n  parallelism, independent review, or substantial context justifies it.\r\n- Dispatch staging, packing, gate-chain invocation, and instrument authorship as units - `builder`\r\n  for a fully specified script, `verifier` for its evidence - each with a brief and an audit like\r\n  any other unit. The commit and the push stay with the Orchestrator.\r\n- A release instrument installs, commits, or runs a mutating tree-wide command, which the\r\n  permission floor bars every role from, so its run is the Orchestrator's tracked command, retained\r\n  with its log as the authoring unit's acceptance evidence. An upload loop that must run inside a\r\n  one-time code's life with the user at the keyboard stays with the Orchestrator whole.\r\n\r\n## Roles\r\n\r\nOne role set, mirrored per provider. Name the role and state its engine in every dispatch, even\r\nwhen the role file already pins it.\r\n\r\n| Job                                      | Claude role (`.claude/agents/`) | Codex role (`.codex/agents/`) | Engine                        |\r\n| ---------------------------------------- | ------------------------------- | ----------------------------- | ----------------------------- |\r\n| Absorption, distillation, scouting       | `grok`                          | `grok`                        | Cursor Grok (bridge)          |\r\n| Creative design and alternatives         | `planner`                       | `planner`                     | Opus 5 (native / bridge)      |\r\n| Design-fit review and audit              | `reviewer`                      | `reviewer`                    | Opus 5 (native / bridge)      |\r\n| Objective analysis and correctness audit | `analyst`                       | `analyst`                     | GPT-5.6 Sol (bridge / native) |\r\n| Nontrivial implementation (objective)    | `sol`                           | `implementer`                 | GPT-5.6 Sol (bridge / native) |\r\n| Nontrivial implementation (subjective)   | `implementer`                   | `opus`                        | Opus 5 (native / bridge)      |\r\n| Bounded primary-source research          | `researcher`                    | `researcher`                  | Grok \u001a Luna \u001a Sonnet          |\r\n| Repository reconnaissance                | `scout`                         | `scout`                       | Grok \u001a Luna \u001a Sonnet          |\r\n| Mechanical conformance evidence          | `checker`                       | `checker`                     | Grok \u001a Luna \u001a Sonnet          |\r\n| Ecosystem evidence                       | `orkestrel`                     | `orkestrel`                   | Sonnet / Terra                |\r\n| Fully specified mechanical unit          | `builder`                       | `builder`                     | Sonnet / Terra                |\r\n| Fully specified app-layer unit           | `application`                   | `application`                 | Sonnet / Terra                |\r\n| Gate evidence                            | `verifier`                      | `verifier`                    | Sonnet / Terra                |\r\n\r\n- A **bridge** role is a cheap driver whose only work is invoking another provider's CLI. It never\r\n  implements, judges, or endorses the result.\r\n- `implementer` names the harness's native implementation lane, so the token means Opus in Claude\r\n  Code and Sol in Codex. An engine-named bridge - `sol`, `opus` - names the other engine. Read a\r\n  role name against the harness you are running in, and state the engine anyway.\r\n- Give every role a file in the scaffold checkout, under `.claude/agents/` and under\r\n  `.codex/agents/`. The role file is where engine, effort, tools, permissions, and charter are\r\n  pinned, and the tool allowlist is what makes the read-only floor real. A role with no file has\r\n  nowhere to pin either. The requirement is the canon repository's alone: a fleet target holds the\r\n  catalog agent and no other role, and a session that dispatches roles starts on scaffold and\r\n  attaches the target.\r\n- Reach every role by its own name. Do not rely on a remembered route.\r\n- `researcher`, `scout`, and `checker` are native lanes for jobs that belong to Grok first.\r\n  Dispatch `grok` with their brief before using them, and use the native role only once the ladder\r\n  has stepped past Grok. Record which step you are on.\r\n- `orkestrel` stays native because it carries the package catalog in its own role file. Sending its\r\n  job to a bench means shipping that catalog across, which costs more than the bench saves.\r\n- A transport contract lives in `.agents/transports/`, not in an agents directory. A harness lists\r\n  its dispatchable agents from that directory, so a contract that is never dispatched sits outside\r\n  it. `.agents/transports/codex.md` is the shared Sol transport contract and\r\n  `.agents/transports/claude.md` the shared Opus transport contract. Neither is a route: `analyst`\r\n  and `sol` are the named Sol bridges, `planner`, `reviewer`, and `opus` the named Opus bridges, and\r\n  each binds its own contract by reference and pins only its route and sandbox.\r\n- Mirroring is by work class, not filename. A transport contract is provider-specific: the Codex\r\n  contract carries the Sol transport the Claude-side bridges follow, the Claude contract carries the\r\n  Opus transport the Codex-side bridges follow, and each side's bridges bind their own.\r\n- Opus and Sol roles use high effort. Native cheap-tier roles use low or medium. Bridge drivers use\r\n  the cheapest tier that can run a CLI.\r\n- Never route orchestration or acceptance across a bridge.\r\n\r\n## Permission floor\r\n\r\nEvery role honours this floor. No dispatch may widen it.\r\n\r\n- Agents run to completion. Constrain only genuine security or destruction risk. Do not gate\r\n  routine work behind approval prompts or turn budgets.\r\n- Read-only roles carry no `Edit` and no `Write`. The tool allowlist is the guarantee. Because\r\n  those roles cannot inspect the tree by writing to it, the Orchestrator supplies the actual diff\r\n  and status evidence in every review dispatch.\r\n- `verifier` has no edit or write tools and never fixes a failure.\r\n- Run one writing role per checkout, on disjoint checkouts, each dispatched from a clean committed\r\n  baseline and each owning disjoint files. In a single checkout that is one writer at a time.\r\n- Treat every shared file as report-only.\r\n- No role commits, pushes, tags, publishes, installs dependencies, or runs a destructive command.\r\n- No role runs `git checkout`, `git restore`, `git stash`, `git reset`, or `git clean`. Each discards\r\n  a working-tree change silently. A role that must undo its own edit undoes exactly that edit.\r\n- A dispatch that has a unit plant a line to prove an instrument can fail names a file the unit under\r\n  verification did not touch, and names how the plant is removed. Check the tree's status before\r\n  choosing the file.\r\n- No role reads, prints, copies, uploads, or packages a secret - `CURSOR_API_KEY`, Codex auth\r\n  files, `.env*`, `.npmrc`, `auth.json`, keys, or tokens.\r\n- Concurrent executors never run tree-wide `format`, lint `--fix`, or `build`. They validate\r\n  read-only and scoped to their own files.\r\n- Keep hooks light. A Stop hook may run cheap changed-file verification such as `git diff --check`.\r\n  It never duplicates the gate suite; gates belong to `verifier`.\r\n\r\n## Context and decomposition\r\n\r\n- Keep the main context at decision level. Send large reads, repository scans, raw logs, diffs,\r\n  and exploratory sweeps to `grok` and consume the distillate.\r\n- Settle a behavioural question by running it, including your own. This binds before a brief is\r\n  written, not only after a report arrives, and it needs no disagreement to trigger it. A round of\r\n  deliberation that a probe would have ended is the expensive failure, and it is invisible because\r\n  it feels like rigour.\r\n- Check an assumption before it enters the plan. An unverified belief the Orchestrator states\r\n  becomes a fact for every unit downstream, and every one of them inherits the error.\r\n- Break a repeating frame deliberately. When several rounds against one subject keep finding the\r\n  same class of defect through a new door, the search is following the frame rather than the\r\n  defect. Bound the scope, then fan out independent lenses over disjoint slices in one pass.\r\n  Parallelism is worth more here for the framing it breaks than for the wall-clock it saves.\r\n- When the recurring class has a direction - each fix relocates it along one stream: a dependency\r\n  chain, a data path, a call chain - the source sits elsewhere on that stream, and deepening the\r\n  current station cannot reach it. Switch from depth to breadth: fan probes over the stream's\r\n  stations in parallel, blind and clean-contexted, as far up and down as the stream runs, to locate\r\n  the source. Then plan downstream from the source with the sweep's map of how far the defect\r\n  reaches, so the remaining work carries a measured bound instead of an open count of rounds. The\r\n  seam budget in `.claude/rules/quality.md` \u0015 Rounds and verdicts states when this fires.\r\n- The subjective and objective lanes are the adversarial pass's FLOOR, not its shape. Where a\r\n  subject has more seams than that pass can attack, fan out one lens per seam over disjoint slices,\r\n  keep every lens blind and\r\n  clean-contexted, and number every slice's claims in one shared sequence. Change the lenses in a\r\n  successor round rather than repeating them.\r\n- Decompose by required context and independently verifiable acceptance criteria, not by task type.\r\n- Send instructions down fully specified. Return findings smaller than the context consumed.\r\n- Parallelize independent work. Serialize dependencies and shared-file contention.\r\n- The Orchestrator owns the plan and every final decision. Design engines propose, writers execute,\r\n  auditors advise.\r\n\r\n## Writing concurrency\r\n\r\nConcurrent executors share a filesystem unless isolated. Follow these rules to prevent clobbered\r\nedits, formatter and build races, cache phantoms, and validation cross-talk.\r\n\r\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}
